### Rules for thinking errors, pointers for facts

When a wrong answer comes from a kind of thinking, give a transferable rule that separates right from wrong; point back to the text only for a missed fact.

A grade-3 reading rulebook requires every wrong-answer reply to name the misconception in the child's own terms, give a way forward and stop short of the answer. The way forward is a criterion when the error is a kind of thinking and a text pointer only when it is a missed fact, because a criterion transfers to the next item and a page reference does not. Its validator holds a reply that is missing, under 6 words, states the answer, shares 6 words in a row with the correct option or repeats the correct-answer reply, and warns above 45 words, none of which tells a criterion from a pointer.

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/artifacts/judgment-corpus/distilled-src/07-SCORING-FEEDBACK-XP.md` (111-113): “**Every wrong-answer reply does exactly three things (R-Q-22)**: names the misconception in the child's own terms, gives a way forward, and stops short of the answer”
- `grade3-reading/artifacts/judgment-corpus/distilled-src/07-SCORING-FEEDBACK-XP.md` (113-115): “The way forward is a criterion (a transferable rule) when the error is a kind of thinking, and a text pointer only when the error is a missed fact; a criterion transfers to the next item, a page reference does not.”
- `grade3-reading/artifacts/judgment-corpus/distilled-src/07-SCORING-FEEDBACK-XP.md` (117-119): “missing reply, <6 words, states answer, 6-word verbatim overlap with the correct option, identical to the correct-answer reply; warns >45 words.”
- `othello-course-renderer-draft/repo/docs/rules/questions-and-assessment.md` (359): “**Rule.** Every wrong-answer reply does three things and no more.”
- `othello-course-renderer-draft/repo/docs/rules/questions-and-assessment.md` (364-367): “Either point at where the text settles it ("Look at the sentences about learning an epic by ear") or hand over the criterion that separates right from wrong here ("A reason comes before the action").”
- `othello-course-renderer-draft/repo/docs/rules/questions-and-assessment.md` (367-369): “A criterion is the better hint of the two when the child's error is about a kind of thinking rather than a missed fact, because it transfers to the next item and a page reference does not.”
- `othello-course-renderer-draft/repo/docs/qa/peter-pan-content-eval/2026-09-28/ADJUDICATION.md` (63): “vocabulary misses need conceptual correction rather than a passage redirect.”

Against: The rule leaves its central judgment to people: "Whether the named misconception is actually right stays a review job." (`grade3-reading/artifacts/judgment-corpus/distilled-src/07-SCORING-FEEDBACK-XP.md`, line 119). The record feedback-that-sends-the-student-back has feedback on a wrong answer say where to look, which this rule keeps for missed facts only.

<sub>criterion · feedback, hints · universal · from g3 · adopted-rule · held · found in [2026-10-12-5-full-crawl](../research/2026-10-12-5-full-crawl/)</sub>

### Say each thing once

Say each thing once and only to teach: no line restating the printed question or anything already said or shown, explaining the obvious, or competing with the learner's action. An automated check warns when a guide line shares 60% or more of its content words with the question beside it.

Review comments on a grade 3 reading course cut lines that repeated what the guide had just said or a label the screen already showed, and a review call removed prompts such as 'now tap the correct answer', which generated content kept producing, as inflating cognitive load and teaching nothing (`grade3-reading/course-renderer/docs/rules/content-and-copy.md`, lines 127-157). An attention audit of the course's screens then made the question print its ask once, with everything else framing it or adding to it, and a lint warns at 60% content-word overlap between a guide line and the question beside it. A QA invariant also bars printing a label, such as a blank's number, that the interface already shows.

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/course-renderer/docs/rules/content-and-copy.md` (129-130): “**Rule.** Delete any line that repeats what the guide has just said or what the screen already shows.”
- `grade3-reading/course-renderer/docs/rules/content-and-copy.md` (144-145): “**Rule.** Do not print "now tap the correct answer" and similar. They inflate cognitive load and teach nothing.”
- `grade3-reading/course-renderer/docs/process/ATTENTION-HIERARCHY.md` (23-24): “The question chrome prints the ask; everything else frames or ADDS”

### Say only what happened

Tell and record only what actually happened: no canned line posing as a response, no step the student didn't take, and an end screen that claims completion, never mastery.

A per-decision audit of one reading lesson's build found a welcome screen that overclaimed contingent listening while giving the same recast whatever the student said, and a receipt screen that asserted out-loud and self-done events that had not happened on the typed paths (`grade3-reading/knowledge_bot/knowledge/kb-0237-u4l2-carbon-copy-build-2026-08-13-to-08-21.qmd`). In the fixed build, a third attempt ends in a release framed as help, never as a solo pass. Nothing promises something the page lacks, a lesson counts as done only once its checks were attempted, help is named as help, and each recap is written for its own item.

### Say what a source can't show

A lesson says plainly what its source cannot show, and models declining an inference the source does not support.

A reading course's lesson-writing playbook makes this its distinctive register: the lesson says plainly what a source or representation cannot show, without hedging into vagueness and without overclaiming, and models honest refusal of unsupported inferences (`grade3-reading/artifacts/g5-course/LESSON-AUTHORING-PLAYBOOK.md`). A course proposal's transfer layer allows teaching students to explain why an option is unsupported (`grade3-reading/artifacts/knowledge-first-reading-course-proposal-2026-07-09/03-transfer-practice-layer.md`).

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/artifacts/g5-course/LESSON-AUTHORING-PLAYBOOK.md` (204): “**Epistemic register — the distinctive G5 note.**”
- `grade3-reading/artifacts/g5-course/LESSON-AUTHORING-PLAYBOOK.md` (206): “vagueness and without overclaiming. Model honest refusal of unsupported inferences.”
- `grade3-reading/artifacts/knowledge-first-reading-course-proposal-2026-07-09/03-transfer-practice-layer.md` (71): “- explain why an option is unsupported;”
- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/courses/apush/review/brainlift.html` (163-164): “Lessons state what a source can establish and what it cannot.”
- `g9/sources/Othello_1_Feedback_DevFactory.md` (391): “REFINE carried a check-and-improve beat and a limit-naming beat”
- `g9/library/text/L36b8ba.txt` (391): “Neither has a new home in the four-step procedure, and the second is the only place in the scaffold that separates what a character believes from what is true.”
- `grade3-reading/_archive/2026-08-13-consolidation/apush-corpus-hardening-20260731-tip.tar.gz` (apush/docs/BRAINLIFT.md inside it) (385-387): “A European map that labels America can demonstrate changing geographic representation. It cannot directly establish Indigenous political boundaries or consent.”

Against: none found

<sub>criterion · wording, stimulus, demand · universal · from g3 · adopted-rule · held · found in [2026-10-12-4-deep-sweep](../research/2026-10-12-4-deep-sweep/), [2026-10-12-5-full-crawl](../research/2026-10-12-5-full-crawl/)</sub>

### Say what the second answer adds

Tell a student what a later answer adds to an earlier one only when the judge is sure, in a fixed authored line, never as a mark. Three lines are written in advance: the answer now rests on the text's own words, makes a sharper claim, or weighs another reading.

When the lesson question is answered again at the end, the lesson engine asks the judge one four-way choice on what the final answer adds to the first: exact words from the text, a sharper claim, another reading, or nothing new (`g9/transformer/src/mechanics/learning-question.js`). A line is shown only for a sure choice other than nothing new; the line is the engine's own and quotes neither answer, and nothing is said about words aimed at the marker.

Quoted:

- `g9/transformer/src/mechanics/learning-question.js` (12): “const MOVED = { A: "It now rests on the text's own words.", B: "It now makes a sharper claim than the first.", C: "It now weighs another reading." };”
- `g9/transformer/src/mechanics/learning-question.js` (71-72): “one Jev choice on what it adds to the first, shown only when Jev is … sure, and never as a mark. The line is ours and quotes neither answer.”
- `g9/transformer/src/mechanics/learning-question.js` (77): “D: "nothing new: it says what first says"”
- `othello-archive/server/tasks/close.mjs` (71): “Name exactly how their thinking moved between the two answers (what is new: a quote, a key word, a second side, a sharper claim).”

Against: The choice is advisory and has no labelled cases. A later run found: a 3D version has the model itself name how the thinking moved between the first and final answers, in its own words held by code to exact quotation of the learner, whenever the final answer is a real attempt, rather than in a fixed line on a sure judgment (`othello-archive/server/tasks/close.mjs`).

### Say why a new word matters

Introduce new words through the lesson's purpose and the whole text they serve, not in isolation, and say when a word carries a theme beyond one character or lesson.

Feedback on a Grade 9 prototype's opening word web asked it to stress that the word matters not only for this lesson but as a theme across the whole text, and the review made rewriting a line that tied the words to one character and one lesson a priority item. Its note calls the pattern vocabulary attached to the lesson's purpose rather than defined in isolation, a move seen in two units, which it says makes it worth stating as a rule.

Quoted:

- `g9/sources/Othello_1_Feedback_DevFactory.md` (224): “can we stress the reason we’re starting with this word is not just for this lesson, but it’s a crucial theme across the course of the text?”
- `g9/sources/Othello_1_Feedback_DevFactory.md` (83): “Rewrite "Today, all of them belong to Iago" so the words are anchored to the whole play rather than to one character and one lesson.”
- `g9/sources/Othello_1_Feedback_DevFactory.md` (215): “vocabulary attached to the lesson’s purpose rather than defined in isolation.”
- `g9/library/text/L36b8ba.txt` (215): “"These words are crucial for today" is the same move as "That is called a betrayal" on the Aladdin Skill panel: vocabulary attached to the lesson’s purpose rather than defined in isolation.”
- `g9/library/text/L36b8ba.txt` (226): “The reason for starting with this word is not just this lesson — it is a crucial theme across the course of the text, and the screen should say so.”
- `g9/library/text/L36b8ba.txt` (82): “Go from the web straight to the Learning Question, framed as "These words are crucial for today."”
- `g9/library/text/L36b8ba.txt` (83): “Rewrite "Today, all of them belong to Iago" so the words are anchored to the whole play rather than to one character and one lesson.”
- `g9/library/text/L5d411f.txt` (10): “Carry these words through the whole play. They are not for one lesson; they return in every act that follows.”

Against: none found

### Scaffolds easier to read than the text

Write instructions, prompts, feedback and anything a lesson's guide says at an easier reading level than the text they support, and keep internal design terms out of them. One course's checklist keeps every passage within 420 to 650 on a reading-level scale and every instruction below that band.

The walkthrough of a built Grade 3 lesson sets child-facing prose at 420–650L, below the passage, warm and without jargon, because the scaffold must be easier to read than the thing it scaffolds (`downloads/SUPERSEDED — see v2.1_ WITW Lesson 1 learning walkthrough v2 (table format).md`, line 38). The internal name of the procedure the lesson teaches never reaches the child; its introduction speaks in what-we-will-do terms instead (`downloads/SUPERSEDED — see v2 (table format)_ WITW Lesson 1 learning walkthrough.md`, line 181). A checklist for the same lessons gives 420–650L as the passage band and puts instructional lines below it (`grade3-reading/artifacts/AlphaRead-QC-Checklist-v2-2026-09-04.md`, line 64).

Quoted:

### Sentences of 25 words at most

Cap every sentence a student must act on, from instructions and questions to hints and feedback, at about 25 words. The readability data the rule cites put a 14-word sentence at about 90% understood and a 43-word sentence under 10%.

The reading project adopted the cap from a request to check the course is age appropriate, citing research that tied sentence length to comprehension at that grade band (R² = .55). It covers instructions, question stems, hints, second hints, reveals and the reply to a wrong answer, with story prose and the taught sentence frames exempt. A validator enforces it on written content, and the live scoring route holds its own generated praise and feedback to the same ceiling, since no static validator can see a line that does not exist until a child answers. The same cap is the practical rule carried to the Grade 9 unit.

### Shared feedback fits every answer

A retry or hint message shown for every wrong answer must be true of every answer it can be shown for, and must not assume how another part was answered.

In reading lessons, retry messages written for one expected wrong answer were shown for every miss: one opened with "Almost" for any miss, including answers nowhere near right, and another named a word the item itself accepted, so one student was told about that word while another who typed it passed. On a cloze, the feedback for one blank assumed the blank before it had been answered correctly.

Quoted:

- `shadow-content-eval/reports/G3-U1-L11-review.md` (52): “It is shown for every miss, including "scary" or "gloomy".”
- `shadow-content-eval/reports/G3-U1-L13-review.md` (67): “A student who typed "scared" is told about "miserable", and a student who typed "miserable" passes.”
- `shadow-content-eval/reports/G3-U2-L1-review.md` (180): “The `depend` feedback on blank 3 (*"That word is already in the gap before this one"*) assumes blank 2 was answered correctly.”
- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/archive/g3-reading-v4/content-for-analysis/BUILD-RULES.md` (651): “true of *presence* only — a stemmed match proves the child said the word, never that they used it”
- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/archive/g3-reading-v4/content-for-analysis/BUILD-RULES.md` (652): “correctly, so no reply may congratulate correct usage.”

Against: none found

### Short feedback, or fix the item

Keep corrective feedback short and aimed at the error; a correction that needs more is new teaching, so fix the item or its placement instead. One later course never lets feedback run past 60 words.

A second-round outside review of a Grade 3 reading course found corrective feedback on many wrong answers but far too long, at times longer than the passage itself, and said a grade-3 child would not read it (`grade3-reading/artifacts/op-taxonomy-calibration-2026-07-10/r2-feedback-docx-converted.txt`). The reading project's audit of its practice principles records the rule that when the feedback needed to correct a response is longer and more complex than the original instruction, the item has crossed the acquisition boundary, so the item or its placement is fixed, not the feedback (`grade3-reading/artifacts/team-direction-2026-09-09/research/02-current-brainlift-spov-audit.md`). A later Grade 5 course's authoring rules set feedback at a 40-word target, never more than 60 (`grade3-reading/artifacts/g5-course/LESSON-AUTHORING-PLAYBOOK.md`). Feedback is never longer or harder than the passage or the teaching it corrects, and its length is checked in code.

Enforced only when more than a fifth of items fail: 'feedback_quality -> 0.0 when >20% of items-with-feedback carry a hard defect.' (`shadow-bench`, git commit 9c9d00c, line 15). The per-option check is 'Report-only by design' (git commit 6e7af07, line 22). The word limits are one team's bar, not a measured threshold.

### Show peers' answers only after committing

Show how other learners answered only after a learner has committed their own answer, and never with names.

Two Grade 9 design canvases set when other learners' answers appear. One shows the live split of how others ruled only after the student commits, under the heading peers after the verdict, and the current one ends on a wrap, peers only after you commit, whose class split is live from the class, shown only after the learner submits and never with names. The built prototype also ends with where the class landed.

Quoted:

- `othello-course-renderer-draft/repo/docs/design/grade-9/canvases/02-case-files.html` (47): “Peers after the verdict”
- `othello-course-renderer-draft/repo/docs/design/grade-9/canvases/02-case-files.html` (47): “The live split of how others ruled appears only after the student commits.”
- `othello-course-renderer-draft/repo/docs/design/grade-9/canvases/03-desktop-othello.html` (38): “Wrap: the argument is the artifact; peers only after you commit”
- `othello-course-renderer-draft/repo/docs/design/grade-9/canvases/03-desktop-othello.html` (38): “Live from your class. Shown only after you submit. Names are never shown.”
- `othello-course-renderer-draft/repo/docs/design/grade-9/prototype/README.md` (31): “**Done**: the argument to keep, the big idea, where the class landed, what's next.”

Against: The prototype's split is not real yet: "The class split on the wrap shows `[X]%` and `[Y]%` placeholders; it needs real response data." (othello-course-renderer-draft/repo/docs/design/grade-9/prototype/README.md, line 42), so the effect of seeing it only after committing is not measured.

### Show that the system is working

Show a visible waiting state whenever the system is working on a learner's answer, so a pause reads as thinking rather than broken.

A Grade 3 reading build's case for approval reports that waiting is now visible: every pause while the guide reads a child's answer shows a spinner, so a child can tell the difference between thinking and broken.

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/archive/g3-reading-v4/CASE-FOR-APPROVAL.md` (154): “**Waiting is now visible.**”
- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/archive/g3-reading-v4/CASE-FOR-APPROVAL.md` (154-155): “pause shows a spinner, so a child can tell the difference between thinking and broken.”

Against: The document states the change and its reason but reports no measurement of its effect.

<sub>criterion · feedback, accessibility · universal · from g3 · adopted-rule · held · found in [2026-10-12-5-full-crawl](../research/2026-10-12-5-full-crawl/)</sub>

### Show the lines that prove the answer

When a question about a text is answered right or its answer is given, mark the lines in the text that prove the answer and bring them into view.

The lesson engine's own lesson view, drawn in an animated version's markup, puts that build's margin line on the lines that prove the answer whenever a reply carries them, and brings them into view (`g9/transformer/web/lesson.js`). The blocks send those lines with a right answer, and with the answer given after the last try in the evidence hunt and the open check (`g9/transformer/src/mechanics/quick-check.js`, `g9/transformer/src/mechanics/evidence-hunt.js`, `g9/transformer/src/mechanics/open-check.js`). The rule has not been tried with readers.

Quoted:

- `g9/transformer/web/lesson.js` (167): “reveal.units: the build's margin line (.marked) on the lines that prove the answer, brought into view.”
- `g9/transformer/web/lesson.js` (242): “if (res.reveal?.units?.length) markLines(res.reveal.units);”
- `g9/transformer/web/lesson.js` (362): “if (res.reveal?.units?.length) markLines(res.reveal.units);”
- `g9/transformer/src/mechanics/quick-check.js` (90): “reveal: correct ? { units: item.evidence } : null”
- `g9/transformer/src/mechanics/evidence-hunt.js` (98): “reveal: { units: item.evidence }, done: true, released: true”
- `g9/transformer/src/mechanics/open-check.js` (108): “reveal: { units: item.lines }, done: true, released: true”

Against: The rule has not been tried with readers.

### Show what the answer is not

When a learner's written answer misses the target, show a short list of what the answer is not, drawn from the lesson's own knowledge bank. One design shows four things the answer is not, at a cost of 0:20.

A Grade 3 reading lesson design's guardrails table answers a step-four write-up that misses the target with a don't-fit list, four things the answer is not, taking 0:20 (`g9/sources/Readventure - Lesson Design and Scaffolding - Updated.docx`, lines 78-80). It notes that the knowledge bank in every inference lesson already holds this content (line 81).

Quoted:

### Show which task holds the learner

When progress is locked, show the learner which task is holding it, along with their position in the lesson and the reading mode they are in. Walkthroughs asked four questions at every transition, among them whether the student could find the next available action.

Research for a Grade 5 reading course's book redesign found that the reading controls confused mode with action and that the footer dots did not show position. The delivered footer states the page position, the current reading mode and the pending action, and names the task holding the Next button. The course's walkthrough protocol checks at every transition whether the student's current task is clear and whether the next available action can be found.

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/visual-findings-2026-09-08/grade5-book-flow/PLAN.md` (12): “reading controls confuse mode with action; footer dots do not communicate position.”
- `grade3-reading/visual-findings-2026-09-08/grade5-book-flow/README.md` (11): “The footer states page position, current reading mode and the pending action.”
- `grade3-reading/visual-findings-2026-09-08/grade5-book-flow/PR-BODY.md` (12): “Show page position, stable reading-mode labels and the task holding Next.”
- `grade3-reading/visual-findings-2026-09-07/review-readiness/WALKTHROUGHS.md` (13): “At each transition check four visual questions: Is the student's current task clear? Can they read all required content? Can they find the next available action? Is new feedback visible or reachable without hiding the task?”

Against: none found

### Simple word classes at the lowest stage

At the lowest stage, name words only by a few simple classes and devices, such as noun, verb or metaphor, and keep finer terms for the top stage. One knowledge base allows six simple labels at its first stage and keeps terms such as tricolon and past participle for its third.

A Grade 9 unit's knowledge base limits its first stage to six simple classes, noun, verb, adjective, adverb, repetition and metaphor, and lets its third stage name tricolon, paradox and past participle (`g9/sources/1. Act 1 - Knowledge Base - Overview.csv`, line 138). Its quality pass found a word labelled a verb that is a past participle, kept the simpler label at the first stage and left the precise term for the third (line 149).

Quoted:

- `g9/sources/1. Act 1 - Knowledge Base - Overview.csv` (138): “Simple classes only at Stage 1: noun, verb, adjective, adverb, repetition, metaphor. Stage 3 may name tricolon, paradox, past participle.”
- `g9/sources/1. Act 1 - Knowledge Base - Overview.csv` (149): “Labelled ""verb"". It is a past participle”
- `g9/sources/1. Act 1 - Knowledge Base - Overview.csv` (149): “Kept as ""verb"" at Stage 1 (simple classes). Name it at Stage 3. Flagged, not changed.”

Against: The case was flagged, not resolved (`g9/sources/1. Act 1 - Knowledge Base - Overview.csv`, line 149), and another record asks lessons to simplify without saying anything false; whether calling a past participle a verb is false is arguable.

### Sparing, consistent emphasis

Use emphasis sparingly and consistently, with no all-caps, because emphasis on one fragment can tell learners that only that fragment needs an answer.

Review feedback of 11 August 2026 on a Grade 5 reference lesson asked for consistent type styling in a modelled response, so that emphasis does not imply learners answer only one fragment, and asked that hints avoid all-caps emphasis and use bold only when sparing emphasis is necessary. The course's instruction-review profile maps both to a criterion that rejects learner-facing language that is over-emphasized, among other faults. The mapping implies no verdict on the current course.

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/courses/g5-reading/review/generated/feedback-2026-08-11-crosswalk.json` (219): “Use consistent type styling so emphasis does not imply that learners answer only one fragment.”
- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/courses/g5-reading/review/generated/feedback-2026-08-11-crosswalk.json` (267): “Avoid all-caps emphasis and use bold only when sparing emphasis is necessary.”
- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/courses/g5-reading/review/generated/instruction-review-profile.json` (350): “Language is inaccurate, developmentally mismatched, inconsistently named, over-emphasized, or incorrectly punctuated.”
- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/courses/g5-reading/review/generated/feedback-2026-08-11-crosswalk.json` (4): “Map every distinct August 11 reviewer observation to the reusable instruction-review criteria without claiming that the current Grade 5 delivery has passed.”

### Stage supports, don't stack them

Bring each support, such as a word bank, hints or sentence starters, onto the screen only at the step that uses it, so no screen opens on many live targets. An audit of 21 student screen types across four lessons of one course led to the rule that no screen may open on fifteen live targets.

The rule is one of seven in an attention standard that a grade 3 reading course codified from an audit of all 21 student-mode screen types in four of its lessons, measured at two screen sizes with per-element pixel shares and a blurred copy of each screen (`grade3-reading/course-renderer/docs/process/ATTENTION-HIERARCHY.md`, lines 3-11). It has the cloze word bank arm when a blank is aimed at, hints unlock after the first attempt and sentence starters wait behind a request, and the standard treats a screen that can't pass it as not done.

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/course-renderer/docs/process/ATTENTION-HIERARCHY.md` (39-42): “**Support is staged, not stacked.** Word banks, hints, and sentence starters mount at the step that uses them: the cloze bank arms when a blank is aimed, hints unlock after the first attempt, starters live behind "Need a starter?". A screen must never open on fifteen live targets.”
- `grade3-reading/course-renderer/docs/process/ATTENTION-HIERARCHY.md` (3-4): “Codified from the WITW UX Attention Audit (2026-08-31; branch `witw-tier3-conformance`), which measured all 21 student-mode screen archetypes”

Against: none found

### Strike the option, not its explanation

When a worked example marks a rejected option, style the option itself and keep the explanation of why it fails fully readable. A 3-pixel red strike line, set on the box holding both the option and its explanation, ran through every line of that explanation in two lessons.

In a modelled-reasoning step the decoration for a rejected candidate, a 3px red line-through, was set on the element holding both the candidate and its explanation, so it was drawn through every line of the explanation the student must read to see why the answer is unsuitable. It appeared in two lessons, through all three candidate steps in one of them, and stayed visible after advancing. The expected fix scopes the decoration to the quoted candidate, and the regression check passes only when the explanations read without strikethrough while the rejected candidate is still clearly identified.

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/visual-findings-2026-09-07/laptop-student-sweep/L1-model-rationale-strikethrough.md` (5): “The student must read that explanation to understand why the answer is unsuitable, but a thick red line runs through its letters.”
- `grade3-reading/visual-findings-2026-09-07/laptop-student-sweep/L1-model-rationale-strikethrough.md` (16): “Expected: the rationale stays readable; rejection styling identifies the candidate without striking through its teaching explanation.”
- `grade3-reading/visual-findings-2026-09-07/laptop-student-sweep/L1-model-rationale-strikethrough.md` (24): “parent decoration is `line-through 3px rgb(209, 81, 58)`.”

- `grade3-reading/visual-findings-2026-09-07/laptop-student-sweep/L1-model-rationale-strikethrough.md` (14): “through all three candidate steps”

- `grade3-reading/visual-findings-2026-09-07/review-readiness/REGRESSIONS.md` (7): “Explanations remain readable without strikethrough; the rejected candidate is still clearly identified.”

### Strip the working before it ships

Strip drafting, self-checking and debug text from an item before a student or a judge sees it. After fields outside the delivery format were dropped, 30 of 30 winning items in a random live sample passed validation.

A draft-verify-revise spec for document-based items expected its verification text to be penalised, since the grader scores a meta-pedagogical voice negatively, as an earlier paraphrase experiment had shown; the generator checklist bans any 'this question tests…' preamble visible to students. A saved winning item failed delivery validation because it still carried pipeline metadata, and after non-schema fields were dropped, 30 of 30 winners in a random live sample passed. The weekly report concluded that debug metadata belongs in archives and logs, not delivered payloads.

Quoted:

- `icloud-work/aptraining/handover/2026-05-01T1039Z_compass_session_close/02_R42_CoVe_DBQ_spec.md` (150): “The verification meta-text WILL be penalized — IB rubric scores presence of meta-pedagogical voice negatively (we saw this in R10 paraphrase-composition).”

<sub>criterion · wording, integrity · universal · from ap · adopted-rule · held · found in [2026-10-12-4-deep-sweep](../research/2026-10-12-4-deep-sweep/), [2026-10-12-5-full-crawl](../research/2026-10-12-5-full-crawl/)</sub>

### Teenagers opt in to re-teaching

With adolescents, don't re-explain what a learner has shown they can do, and offer re-teaching rather than push it, since repeating what teens think they know feels infantilizing.

The Grade 9 research brief, from a published review read in full, reports that middle adolescents become more alert to whether they are treated with respect and accorded high status, and that interventions repeating what teens think they already know feel infantilizing and tend to fail. It draws two rules for the lesson's guide: never re-explain something the student has already shown they can do, and make re-teaching opt-in, offered as a walkthrough, rather than pushed on every miss. The Grade 9 strategy names being talked down to as the biggest risk in porting a Grade 3 lesson.

Quoted:

- `othello-course-renderer-draft/repo/docs/design/grade-9/research/learners-voice-guide.md` (13): “Interventions that repeat things teens think they already know feel infantilizing and tend to fail.”
- `othello-course-renderer-draft/repo/docs/design/grade-9/research/learners-voice-guide.md` (16): “Re-teaching should be opt-in ("Want a walkthrough?"), not pushed on every miss.”
- `othello-course-renderer-draft/repo/docs/design/grade-9/strategy/grade-9-strategy.md` (7): “Grade 9 readers punish being talked down to, and that is the biggest risk in porting a Grade 3 lesson.”

Published: [Yeager, Dahl & Dweck 2018](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5758430/)

Against: The source is a review and a hypothesis built on randomized trials, not a test of reading lessons (`othello-course-renderer-draft/repo/docs/design/grade-9/research/learners-voice-guide.md`, line 15). For young learners the reading project keeps skipping instruction out of the learner's own hands, so the rule is for older learners.

### The task leads every screen

Make the task the most prominent thing on each screen, checked on the rendered screen, because a text-pattern check can pass a screen where supporting material buries the task. On one screen, the passage outweighed the learner's task 4:1.

The reading course's quality gates list a passage outweighing the child's task 4:1 among the failures visible only when a screen is rendered and in motion (`grade3-reading/ti-courseplans/docs/key-quality-gates.md`, line 27). They now require the primary task to win a squint test on blurred screenshots, at least 25% of the viewport on stage screens and no smaller than the largest competitor on work screens, enforced with pixels because a tripwire-only validator once passed a failing screen (the same file, lines 86-89).

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/ti-courseplans/docs/key-quality-gates.md` (88-89): “enforce with pixels, not seam greps: a tripwire-only validator once passed a failing screen).”
- `grade3-reading/ui-audit-2026-09-08/BEAD-REGISTER.json` (1750): “Both requested four-clue and nested two-clue first arrivals put the current task above the bank; wrong/recovery/quiet controls accepted.”

<sub>criterion · stem, evaluation · universal · from g3 · measured · held · found in [2026-10-12-4-deep-sweep](../research/2026-10-12-4-deep-sweep/), [2026-10-12-5-full-crawl](../research/2026-10-12-5-full-crawl/)</sub>

### A way back after three misses

After three misses in a row, a lesson still gives useful correction and a way to recover, never just an answer to copy or a dead end.

The acceptance criteria a Grade 5 reading course set itself before it could be called student-ready include a frustration-recovery probe, which shows what happens after three consecutive misses. The flow passes only if it gives useful correction and a recoverable path, not answer-copying or abandonment. The course's BrainLift sets the same direction for any miss: correction, a parallel check, delayed retest, spaced return and a capped retry, so that a wrong response reopens instruction.

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/_archive/2026-08-13-consolidation/dirty-state/ti-courseplans-untracked.tar.gz` (courses/g5-reading/STUDENT_READY_CRITICISM_FRAMEWORK.qmd inside it) (279-281): “- A frustration-recovery probe shows what happens after three consecutive misses: the flow must give useful correction and a recoverable path, not answer-copying or abandonment.”
- `grade3-reading/_archive/2026-08-13-consolidation/g5-full-course-worktree-full-tree.tar.gz` (g5-reading-v2/docs/BRAINLIFT.md inside it) (250): “| Miss flow | Correction, parallel check, delayed retest, spaced return, capped retry | A wrong response must reopen instruction, not produce feedback-plus-lockout. |”

Against: A rule on record reveals the answer after two tries so that a student who never gets it right can still finish (two-tries-then-the-answer); a reveal the student simply copies is what this probe rules out, so the two pull against each other.

<sub>criterion · feedback, practice · universal · from g3 · adopted-rule · contested · found in [2026-10-12-5-full-crawl](../research/2026-10-12-5-full-crawl/)</sub>

### Explain the key and every wrong option

Give every item an explanation that shows why its key is right and why each wrong option is wrong, rather than restating the answer.

An AP item benchmark's judge asks whether an explanation teaches why the answer is correct and addresses why the other options are wrong, and fails one that just restates the answer (`icloud-work/incept/ap-benchmark-standalone/ap_benchmark/prompts/base.py`, lines 98-102). Its calibration set includes a failing case whose explanation is the answer repeated (`icloud-work/incept/ap-benchmark-standalone/ap_benchmark/calibration/gold_standard.json`, line 365). A reading generator stores a separate explanation for the right answer and for each distractor (`icloud-work/incept/sat-reading-generation/README.md`, lines 210-213). This is for when the answer is revealed; after a first wrong try, feedback says only where to look. Check in code that every item has an explanation.

Quoted:

Against: Whether to explain each wrong option as well is contested. The local rubric asks '- Does it address why other options are wrong?' (`icloud-work/evaluator/ap_benchmark/prompts/base.py`, line 101). The later pipeline told the writer 'Prefer one concise explanation of why the keyed answer is correct instead of option-by-option rationales.' (`icloud-work/evaluatorML/.agent/memory/e2e_pipeline/iterate_repair_to_100.py`, line 194) and "- Do not use option-by-option explanation text; explain only the keyed answer's reasoning." (line 243). An explanation can itself be wrong: an answer letter that disagreed with its explanation failed 7 of 30 questions in a cross-check by another model ("This bug caused 7 out of 30 questions (23%) to fail", `icloud-work/ap-social-studies-generator/docs/archive/MCQ_BUG_HISTORY.md`, line 6), so each explanation needs its own consistency check. The value of an explanation is asserted in these files, not measured.

<sub>criterion · feedback, distractors · universal · from ap · adopted-rule · contested · found in [2026-10-12-4-deep-sweep](../research/2026-10-12-4-deep-sweep/), [2026-10-12-5-full-crawl](../research/2026-10-12-5-full-crawl/), [2026-10-12-6-transformer-ideas](../research/2026-10-12-6-transformer-ideas/)</sub>

### Keep the attempt, require a fresh correction

After a wrong answer, keep the attempt visible, diagnose its misconception, and pass only the learner's own fresh correction, never an auto-filled answer or a repeated stale selection.

Three rule sets for the reading courses agree. A lesson-acceptance checklist asks that a wrong answer preserve the attempt, diagnose the misconception and require a fresh correction; a Grade 5 teaching-flow release lists preserved wrong work, misconception-specific repair and a required fresh correction among what it proves at source and test level; and a course-experience preflight's outcome rule has feedback diagnose the submitted response, preserve work, withhold hints until an attempt and require correction or retry. None of them records an effect on learning.

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/_archive/2026-08-13-consolidation/council-human-handover-2026-08-13/04_HUMAN_CHECK_LESSONS.md` (175): “- [ ] A wrong answer preserves the attempt, diagnoses the misconception, and requires a fresh correction.”
- `grade3-reading/_archive/2026-08-13-consolidation/adversarial-course-council/initiatives/02-g5-feedback-and-teaching-flow-hardening/HANDOVER.md` (46): “- Wrong work is preserved, misconception-specific repair is shown, and a fresh correction is required.”
- `grade3-reading/_archive/2026-08-13-consolidation/top-level-docs/HANDOVER_COURSE_EXPERIENCE_PREFLIGHT_2026-08-11.qmd` (206): “6. **Respond to the learner's actual thinking.** Feedback diagnoses the submitted response, preserves work, withholds hints until an attempt, and requires correction/retry.”
- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/docs/G5_STUDENT_EXPERIENCE_RELEASE_GATE.qmd` (81): “Wrong A remains blocked; a distinct wrong B reveals support but remains blocked; only a fresh learner-selected correct retest exposes Next.”
- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/docs/GRADE_5_REVIEW_NOTES.qmd` (44): “two-error support that never auto-fills or unlocks the answer and always requires a fresh retest;”
- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/docs/G5_STUDENT_EXPERIENCE_RELEASE_GATE.qmd` (65-67): “4. The retry path did not require two distinct wrong attempts, answer support, and a fresh explicit correct selection. An implementation could therefore reveal and advance without a genuine retest.”
- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/docs/GRADE_5_REVIEW_NOTES.qmd` (22): “one primary action, targeted repair, and successful retest before progression;”

Wrong work stays visible; correction is fresh.** A wrong attempt remains displayed (preserved, diagnosed) and passing requires a fresh learner-made correct action — the UI never auto-fills or lets the same stale selection pass”

- `grade3-reading/visual-findings-2026-09-08/grade5-book-flow/README.md` (43): “The revised editor correctly preserved the answer and kept Next gated.”
- `grade3-reading/visual-findings-2026-09-08/grade5-book-flow/PR-BODY.md` (12): “Preserve submitted Grade 5 answers for revision after misses or service failures.”

Against: A draft acceptance ladder for written answers offers one retry and then lets the student proceed anyway, queuing the attempt for review (`grade3-reading/ti-courseplans/scripts/lane-tools/drafts/m5-acceptance-rules.md`, lines 77-78), and another rule on record gives the answer after two tries. Two other builds release or settle after two tries instead. The lesson engine's evidence hunt and debate "now release after two tries" (`g9/transformer/v02/QA.md`, line 141), and a Grade 3 sample player settles graded items on the second try and records the miss separately: "It always completes by attempt 2 whether or not the child fixed the answer" (`grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/archive/g3-reading-v4/content-for-analysis/CONTENT-SCHEMA.md`, line 326).

<sub>criterion · feedback, practice · universal · from g3 · adopted-rule · contested · found in [2026-10-12-5-full-crawl](../research/2026-10-12-5-full-crawl/)</sub>

### Narrate between the sections of a long text

Narrate a long text read in parts: open each part with a short line on what to watch for, review it afterwards, and summarise at the end. The same format note heads all four reading texts of one Grade 9 unit.

Every reading text in a Grade 9 unit opens with a format note: a narrator speaks between sections, feeding forward before a section, reviewing back after it and summarising at the end, while section headings stay hidden and a simple divider marks each break. The forward feeds point readers at what to look for, such as deciding whether to believe a speaker or looking for one word.

Quoted:

- `g9/sources/Act 1 reading texts/Act 1/1. 1.1 - Iago_s Introduction - Text.docx` (4): “narrates between sections of the text: feeding forward before a section, reviewing back after it, and summarizing at the end. Section headings are not shown to students; a simple divider marks each break.”
- `g9/sources/Act 1 reading texts/Act 1/1. 1.1 - Iago_s Introduction - Text.docx` (16): “As you read, decide whether you believe him.”
- `g9/sources/Act 1 reading texts/Act 1/1. 1.1 - Iago_s Introduction - Text.docx` (102): “Look for the word "flag."”
- `grade3-reading/artifacts/arc-audit/REGISTER-2026-09-15.md` (168): “the per-chunk reading 'purpose' banner (D7.27 / D12.2 motivate-then-orient) is present on 11 of 14 story chunks across the precedent lessons L1/L2/L4 and on 0 of 12 in L5/L6/L7.”
- `grade3-reading/artifacts/arc-audit/REGISTER-2026-09-15.md` (168): “it is a real pedagogical scaffold, not polish, so this is FIX-NOW.”
- `grade3-reading/artifacts/arc-audit/REGISTER-2026-09-15.md` (173): “Do NOT touch chunk-1 of any lesson (its framing lives in the p7 goal page, matching L1 p8 / L2 p6 / L4 p6). All strings verified: no em-dash/semicolon/parenthesis/ALL-CAPS, one child-actionable sentence, under 25 words.”
- `grade3-reading/artifacts/arc-audit/REGISTER-2026-09-15.md` (186): “F10 (nine purposes + clips)”
- `grade3-reading/artifacts/arc-audit/REGISTER-2026-09-16.md` (81): “the JSON has escalating purpose statements per chunk (p8: character observation → p10: cause/belief → p12: mood/craft)”
- `grade3-reading/artifacts/arc-audit/REGISTER-2026-09-15.md` (181): “The `purpose` field also feeds the core.tsx:701 self-read wait timer, so the timed read-through will lengthen slightly by design.”
- `grade3-reading/knowledge_bot/knowledge/kb-0230-late-sept-incidental-findings.qmd` (39): “They take away from the reading flow.”
- `grade3-reading/knowledge_bot/knowledge/kb-0230-late-sept-incidental-findings.qmd` (41): “the `passage.purpose` line printed and spoken above every story part”
- `grade3-reading/knowledge_bot/knowledge/kb-0230-late-sept-incidental-findings.qmd` (45-46): “nine `purpose` + `purpose_voiceover` fields deleted per lesson across L4-L7 + G5-U1-L1, nine clips retired”

Against: Some forward feeds also tell learners what the next section contains before they read it: "In this next section, you'll read that Othello turned those men down and chose someone else." (`g9/sources/Act 1 reading texts/Act 1/1. 1.1 - Iago_s Introduction - Text.docx`, line 38), which the record `no-spoilers-of-what-comes-later` forbids. A ruling of 30 September deleted the lines from four of the course's lessons and one in another course because "They take away from the reading flow." (kb-0230-late-sept-incidental-findings.qmd lines 39-46), and they lengthen the timed read-through (REGISTER-2026-09-15.md line 181).

<sub>criterion · sequence, stimulus · domain · from g3, g9 · adopted-rule · contested · found in [2026-10-12-5-full-crawl](../research/2026-10-12-5-full-crawl/)</sub>

### Offer help, never pick the answer

After repeated misses on a search or a used-up hint ladder, offer help through one button the student must tap to accept, and never pick the answer unasked. After four missed taps on a hunt or hint ladder, help came as a single button the child had to tap to accept.

A ruling for a Grade 3 reading course set that after four missed taps on any hunt or hint ladder, the lesson's guide offers help with a single button the child must tap to accept, and never marks the answer unprompted. It was first set on clue hunts and then extended to every used-up ladder before an automatic pick, so every backstop that picked the answer for the child after a number of tries now sits behind that one offer. No outcome was measured.

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/ti-courseplans/courses/g3-reading/iteration-6/distilled/05-UI-UX-PRINCIPLES.md` (95-96): “After four missed taps on any hunt or ladder, Scout offers to help with a single button; the child must tap to accept, and Scout never marks the answer unprompted.”
- `grade3-reading/ti-courseplans/courses/g3-reading/iteration-6/distilled/05-UI-UX-PRINCIPLES.md` (100-101): “Every `reveal_after_tries` backstop that picked FOR the child is now gated behind the one ScoutOffer flow.”
- `grade3-reading/ti-courseplans/courses/g3-reading/iteration-6/distilled/07-SCORING-FEEDBACK-XP.md` (223-224): “during-reading checks get two attempts then a reveal) and the §3 no-reveal rule above give opposite instructions on the exact item”

Against: The record two-tries-then-the-answer gives the answer after two tries so that a student who never gets it right can still finish. The same course records an unresolved conflict for single-select checks: "during-reading checks get two attempts then a reveal) and the §3 no-reveal rule above give opposite instructions on the exact item" (grade3-reading/ti-courseplans/courses/g3-reading/iteration-6/distilled/07-SCORING-FEEDBACK-XP.md, lines 223-224).

<sub>criterion · hints, practice · universal · from g3 · adopted-rule · contested · found in [2026-10-12-5-full-crawl](../research/2026-10-12-5-full-crawl/)</sub>

### Praise the process, never the person

Praise and encouragement name the specific process or strategy the learner used, never the person, stay truthful and never slip in the answer.

The grade-3 rulebook's register with the child says to praise the process, never the person, never to praise effort on something easy, and never to make points or praise the reason to read. A reading quality canon asks that uptake name the strategy the child used, as in 'I like how you used what you know about lions to decide…', praising the process, not the answer.

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/artifacts/judgment-corpus/distilled-src/03-INSTRUCTION-DESIGN.md` (246-247): “Praise the PROCESS, never the person; never praise effort on something easy.”
- `grade3-reading/artifacts/judgment-corpus/distilled-src/03-INSTRUCTION-DESIGN.md` (248-249): “The reason to read stays inside the text — points and praise are never the reason.”
- `grade3-reading/artifacts/u4l2-build/g5-audit/P2-RULESET.md` (457): “Uptake names the strategy the child used ('I like how you used what you know about lions to decide…'), praising the process not the answer.”
- `g9/transcript/chat-transcript.redacted.txt` (4332): “Motivator: encourage a specific useful effort or strategy, without giving away the answer.”
- `g9/transcript/chat-transcript.redacted.txt` (3097): “The Motivator supports persistence through specific, truthful encouragement without quietly supplying the interpretation.”
- `g9/transcript/chat-transcript.redacted.txt` (2866): “Helps students re-engage without doing the reasoning for them. Encouragement should remain truthful and age-appropriate.”

Against: A grade 9 lesson engine gives every line a student reads one plain voice with no praise at all (the record one-plain-voice-for-every-line), so whether a line may praise differs between courses; the two fit only when the encouragement names an effort or strategy rather than praising. Untested with students.

<sub>criterion · feedback, wording · universal · from g3, g9 · adopted-rule · contested · found in [2026-10-12-5-full-crawl](../research/2026-10-12-5-full-crawl/)</sub>

### Teaching points apart from quiz points

Where a course pays points both for teaching steps and for graded questions, keep the two apart, so a poor quiz never cancels what was earned while learning. A child who works through all 18 sample lessons keeps the teaching part's 252 points however the graded questions go.

A Grade 3 reading sample's points rubric, whose rules are copied from the live course's rubric, splits each lesson into a teaching part, which pays a small fixed amount for each teaching step the child finishes, and a graded part, which pays for focused minutes only if enough of the reading questions are right. The two pay separately and never mix, so a child who works hard through the teaching and then does poorly on the questions keeps everything earned while learning. A step pays only once every part asking for an answer is answered, and reading, watching a model and reflecting pay nothing, since points are for work the child produced that can be checked.

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/archive/g3-reading-v4/XP-RUBRIC.md` (31): “A lesson has a teaching part and a graded part. They pay separately and never mix.”
- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/archive/g3-reading-v4/XP-RUBRIC.md` (39): “keeps everything they earned while learning”
- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/archive/g3-reading-v4/XP-RUBRIC.md` (33): “pays a small, fixed amount for each teaching step the child finishes.”

Against: A Grade 5 release contract pays no points per answer and counts them once, at a successful finish that meets a 90 percent checkpoint, from whole focused minutes: "A correct response records immediate progress and feedback; it does not award per-answer XP." (grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/docs/G5_STUDENT_EXPERIENCE_RELEASE_GATE.qmd, line 37). The records points-for-focused-time-not-answers and mastery-only-for-verified-learning hold that points come once at the finish and that low-accuracy work earns nothing, which a teaching stream paid however the quiz goes does not meet.

<sub>criterion · practice, feedback · domain · from g3 · adopted-rule · contested · found in [2026-10-12-5-full-crawl](../research/2026-10-12-5-full-crawl/)</sub>

### Two tries, then the answer

Two tries, then the answer; otherwise a student who never gets it right can't finish.

The rule comes from a 3D version of the lesson, and the engine uses it in its written checks, key words, evidence hunt
and debate. Before the runtime passed the attempt count, the release never fired in sessions; after it, all four
scripted readers finished a 15-block lesson (`g9/transformer/v02/QA.md`, "Found and fixed in integration" and
"Grade 3 quality").

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/knowledge_bot/knowledge/kb-0183-copy-question-quality-rulings.qmd` (135-136): “a single-select (`mcq`) item is *never* revealed after N tries”
- `grade3-reading/knowledge_bot/knowledge/kb-0183-copy-question-quality-rulings.qmd` (136-138): “because a short, closed option list lets the child eliminate their way to the answer — revealing it mid-reasoning stops the exact thinking the item exists to provoke.”
- `grade3-reading/knowledge_bot/knowledge/kb-0183-copy-question-quality-rulings.qmd` (138-139): “Every other gated question type (open response, `sequence`, `match`, `inline_choice`) keeps a tries-ceiling and ends in a reveal”

### Check feedback for the answer reworded

Check wrong-option feedback for the right answer said in other words, which a word-match check misses and a truth check never asks about. The code check fires only on a run of at least three of the right option's own words, so a shipped quick check whose wrong-option feedback gave the answer in other words passed it.

The shared code check flags a wrong option's feedback only when it repeats a run of the right option's words as long as the option, with a floor of three words, so it cannot catch a key of one or two words or any key said in other words. A quick check on a poem shipped ready with two wrong-option feedback lines that gave the keyed answer in other words, one telling the reader who the poem's speaker is, although its prompt says such feedback must not name the right answer and its judge asks nothing about feedback. In a remade film check, two such lines were judged only on whether they were true (0.72, 0.95), while a later draft that repeated the option's own words was caught.

- `g9/transformer/src/mechanics/util.js` (116): “sharedRun(f, c.options[c.answer]) >= Math.max(3, words(c.options[c.answer]).length)”

Against: Whether a line names the answer in other words is itself a judgment, so a paraphrase check would need person-labelled cases before it holds a block. Pointing back to what was said is what the prompt asks for, so the line may count as a hint rather than a give-away: "wrong option's sends the viewer back to what was said without using the right option's words" (`g9/transformer/src/mechanics/video-essay.js`, line 54).

### Check leaks by words only the key carries

Check hints and wrong-answer replies for leaks in code: none may carry a word only the key or its criterion's answers carry, not counting the criterion's framing words. A leak check that counted the criterion's framing words 'reason' and 'any' withheld a hint and left a child on a third miss with only the question restated.

A reading course checks leaks in code: an item-statistics review collects each criterion's answer words of more than 4 characters and looks for them in that criterion's hints (`grade3-reading/artifacts/peter-pan-standing-2026-09-11/readiness-l5-2026-09-17/sim-review/lens-item-stats.md`), and its feedback validator holds any wrong-answer reply that names or paraphrases the key, quotes six words of the correct option, or names a word only the key carries (`grade3-reading/artifacts/feedback-inceptbench-2026-09-30/BUILD-SPEC-batch.md`). A merge then changed what a child got on a third miss: the old check had flagged 'reason' and 'any', words from the criterion's own framing, not from any answer, and withheld the second hint, leaving only the question restated; the version that stopped counting such words gave the hint (`grade3-reading/artifacts/peter-pan-standing-2026-09-11/readiness-2026-09-16/pr263-merge/RED-REGISTER.md`).

### Check narration against the key idea

Check every line of narration against the lesson's key idea, because a single line can contradict the thesis the lesson is teaching.

A quality pass on a Grade 9 unit's knowledge base found a guide line calling a character a dutiful daughter, not a rebellious one, which undercut the lesson's thesis that she is both defiant and dutiful (`g9/sources/1. Act 1 - Knowledge Base - Overview.csv`, line 148). The line was changed to one that keeps both sides, that her defiance is respectful, not reckless, and the case marked fixed.

Quoted:

- `g9/sources/1. Act 1 - Knowledge Base - Overview.csv` (148): “line ""she is a dutiful daughter, not a rebellious one"" undercut the lesson's defiant AND dutiful thesis. Changed to ""her defiance is respectful, not reckless."" Fixed.”

Against: none found

<sub>method · stage: review · alignment, wording, review · universal · from g9 · adopted-rule · held · found in [2026-10-12-5-full-crawl](../research/2026-10-12-5-full-crawl/)</sub>

### Check the negatives around a quoted line

When narration paraphrases or embeds a quoted line, check that its negatives still say what the line says, since a carried-over negative can make a double negative that reverses it.

A quality pass on a Grade 9 unit's knowledge base found guide narration that kept a quoted line's 'cannot' beside a 'not' of its own, a double negative that reversed what the line says (`g9/sources/1. Act 1 - Knowledge Base - Overview.csv`, line 144). The line itself already carries two negatives (`g9/sources/Act 1 reading texts/Act 1/1. 1.1 - Iago_s Introduction - Text.docx`, lines 68-69). The narration was reworded to a positive form and the case marked fixed.

Quoted:

- `g9/sources/1. Act 1 - Knowledge Base - Overview.csv` (144): “originally said ""not all masters 'cannot be truly followed'"", a double negative that reverses the line. Changed to ""not all masters can be 'truly followed.'"" Fixed.”
- `g9/sources/Act 1 reading texts/Act 1/1. 1.1 - Iago_s Introduction - Text.docx` (68-69): “We cannot all be masters, nor all masters Cannot be truly followed.”

Against: none found

### Quote the learner word for word

When feedback quotes the learner, check in code that every quoted phrase is the learner's own words in order or the text's, and never shorten or belittle what they wrote.

A 3D version's end-of-lesson reflection sets the learner's first and final answers side by side and has the model name how the thinking moved, quoting the learner only word for word and never shortening or belittling an answer. Code then checks every quoted phrase against the learner's two answers and the scene and scans for belittling phrases such as 'only said'; a reply that breaks a rule is written again once with its fault named, and if it still breaks one, only its clean sentences are kept or a fixed line replaces it.

Quoted:

- `othello-archive/server/tasks/close.mjs` (9): “must be the student's own words, word for word.”
- `othello-archive/server/tasks/close.mjs` (73): “Quoting rules: when you quote the student, copy their words EXACTLY and in order, inside double quotes. Never shorten or summarize one of their answers into a few words, and never belittle an answer ("only", "just", "merely", "simply" said or claimed): each answer is given above in full, and the first one was a fair start.”
- `othello-archive/server/tasks/close.mjs` (79): “which is not their words in that order”
- `othello-archive/server/tasks/close.mjs` (88): “still wrong: never misrepresent the student. Keep only the sentences that break no rule; otherwise say plainly what changed”

Against: Retrieval shows a learner's earlier answer back with taught-word spellings corrected (`othello-archive/src/retrieval/Recall.tsx`, line 9, "spellings corrected, so a slip is not carried forward"), so the same build does not always show the learner's words exactly as written.

### Set each grade's voice on purpose

Set each grade's voice, tone, praise, feedback length and pace on purpose for its learners' age, and never let a missing setting fall back to another grade's default. A Grade 5 lesson almost shipped in grade 4's child voice and storytime pace, which the code's own comment called silent and plausible.

The reading project's audio conventions found no grade 9 entry in its voice table, so new clips would silently fall through to grade 4's child voice and storytime pace, the same mistake that almost shipped for Grade 5 (`grade3-reading/knowledge_bot/knowledge/kb-0194-g3-book-audio-clip-conventions.qmd`). A Grade 5 rebuild passed every content validator and its unit test, yet its live page broke because a helper hard-coded grade 3 (`grade3-reading/knowledge_bot/knowledge/kb-0193-g3-book-grade-agnostic-vs-specific-g9-checklist.qmd`). The same transfer checklist found that the Grade 5 build's register (no "You did it!", no medals or confetti, ≤40-word feedback, hedging allowed) was a content standard that lived in the page files, never in the renderer, and it puts a new grade's own register standard first. Keep each grade's register in a standard of its own, apart from the shared code; a fallback to another grade's default looks plausible and is wrong.

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/knowledge_bot/knowledge/kb-0194-g3-book-audio-clip-conventions.qmd` (72-77): “**No grade-9 entry exists.** Without one, G9 clips would silently fall through to `DEFAULT_VOICES`/`DEFAULT_STYLE` = grade 4's child voice and storytime pace — "silent and plausible," per this same file's own comment about the identical mistake almost shipping for Grade 5 ("a ten-year-old reading about salinity gradients would have been read to as if this were a bedtime story").”
- `grade3-reading/knowledge_bot/knowledge/kb-0193-g3-book-grade-agnostic-vs-specific-g9-checklist.qmd` (25-28): “a G5 rebuild passed every content validator and its unit test, yet the live page silently broke, because `bookCompoundResume.ts`'s `frameBlankInput()` hard-coded `grade: 3`”
- `grade3-reading/knowledge_bot/knowledge/kb-0193-g3-book-grade-agnostic-vs-specific-g9-checklist.qmd` (122-127): “**Register/content differences are separate from mechanism differences.** The build spec's "Register (Grade 5)" section (no "You did it!", no medals/confetti, ≤40-word feedback, epistemic hedging allowed, more writing than G3, retell demands taught terms) is entirely a CONTENT authoring standard, not a code change — it lived in the page files' prose, never in the renderer.”
- `grade3-reading/knowledge_bot/knowledge/kb-0193-g3-book-grade-agnostic-vs-specific-g9-checklist.qmd` (134-136): “**Register/pedagogy standard first**, separate from mechanism: define a G9-appropriate voice/tone doc (the G5 build's §3 register rules are the template for this document's shape, not its content — G9 needs its own,”

### Test feedback on the learner who missed

Test feedback on learners who just answered wrong: it works if they can say what went wrong and where to reread, and its mere presence proves nothing.

A Grade 5 reading course's student-readiness criteria test feedback with Grade 5 readers or a strict stand-in: after a wrong answer, the student must be able to identify what went wrong and where to reread within a short interval. The framework's evidence is that an earlier round found feedback too long, sometimes longer than the passage, with capitalisation defects and sometimes generic, while a count of feedback present could not show whether a child could use it. Its fix also audits feedback for brevity, correctness, failure-mode specificity and grade-level wording.

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/_archive/2026-08-13-consolidation/dirty-state/ti-courseplans-untracked.tar.gz` (courses/g5-reading/STUDENT_READY_CRITICISM_FRAMEWORK.qmd inside it) (276): “Feedback is tested with Grade 5 readers”
- `grade3-reading/_archive/2026-08-13-consolidation/dirty-state/ti-courseplans-untracked.tar.gz` (courses/g5-reading/STUDENT_READY_CRITICISM_FRAMEWORK.qmd inside it) (276-278): “after a wrong answer, the student can identify what went wrong and where to reread within a short interval.”
- `grade3-reading/_archive/2026-08-13-consolidation/dirty-state/ti-courseplans-untracked.tar.gz` (courses/g5-reading/STUDENT_READY_CRITICISM_FRAMEWORK.qmd inside it) (183): “`kb-0086` says feedback was too long, sometimes longer than the passage, had capitalization defects, and sometimes remained generic. G5 feedback count can be present without proving child usability. | Audit feedback for brevity, correctness, failure-mode specificity, and grade-level wording. Presence count is not enough. |”

Against: none found

### Write the headline after the verdict

Compose a feedback headline only after the verdict exists, from the same writer, because praise written before the verdict can congratulate a wrong answer above its own correction. In one build's vocabulary say-back, wrong answers were congratulated above their correction until a single writer composed both; a new gate holds it at 19 of 19.

In a Grade 3 reading build's vocabulary say-back, a child who gave a wrong answer was congratulated, 'Nice! That word is now yours', with the correction printed underneath. The cause was ordering: the congratulation was written before the verdict existed, so it could not have agreed with it. The panel now has a single writer, so the headline cannot contradict the judgement below it, and a new gate holds the fix at 19/19, measured on the build on 2026-07-30.

Quoted:

- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/archive/g3-reading-v4/CASE-FOR-APPROVAL.md` (124): “a child who gave a *wrong* answer was being congratulated”
- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/archive/g3-reading-v4/CASE-FOR-APPROVAL.md` (126): “written before the verdict existed, so it could not possibly have agreed with it.”
- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/archive/g3-reading-v4/CASE-FOR-APPROVAL.md` (127): “single writer and the headline cannot contradict the judgement below it.”
- `grade3-reading/edullm-review-hub/course-packages/archive/g3-reading-v4/CASE-FOR-APPROVAL.md` (128): “`vocab_verdict_check`, **19/19**, measured on the build, driven locally, 2026-07-30.”

Against: none found
